## Sheet: Metadata
| LME_034 - Bay of Bengal |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Field | Value | Notes / source |
| Unit ID | LME_034 | Sea Around Us region identifier |
| Transfer efficiency | 0.1 | Used by every SPPR formula |
| Year | 2019 | Latest year common to all 84 datasets |
| Region type | LME | Sea Around Us spatial classification |
| Catch scope | Landings + Discards | Reported + unreported; all sectors, gears, entities, and end uses |
| SPPR formula | (1 / TE)^(TL - 1) | TE is the Metadata!B5 value |
| PPR formula | Catch tonnes × SPPR | Unit: tonnes primary-production equivalent |
| Wet-weight/carbon divisor | Not applied | The paper's /9 conversion is intentionally omitted |
| Primary TL source | 2020 supplement MeanTL | No SPPR regression from the supplement is used |
| Secondary TL source | Sea Around Us exploited organisms | Exact taxon matches, followed by documented fallbacks |
| Catch data URL | https://www.seaaroundus.org/data/ | Frozen archive: raw_data/SAU_downloads/LME_034-catch.zip |
| Method source | Pauly & Christensen (1995) | Nature 374:255-257 |

| Guénette, S. (2013) An exploratory ecosystem model of the Bay of Bengal Large Marine Ecosystem. BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09, Bay of Bengal Large Marine Ecosystem Project, 62 pp. | Bay of Bengal (1978) | Oceanic sharks | 4.22 | Taxonomic (pelagic/oceanic sharks) | Isurus paucus, Isurus oxyrinchus, Alopias vulpinus, Alopias pelagicus, Prionace glauca, Carcharhinus longimanus, Sphyrna lewini, Sphyrna mokarran, Sphyrna zygaena, Carcharhinus falciformis, Alopias superciliosus, Hexanchus griseus, Rhincodon typus, Pteroplatytrygon violacea, Dalatias licha, Echinorhinus brucus, 5 Centrophorus spp., Centroscyllium ornatum, Pseudotriakis microdon, Pseudocarcharias kamoharai, Carcharhinus amblyrhynchoides (Appendix A1.3). | Explicit species membership from Appendix A1.3 ('Functional groups composition', PDF pp.43-45), cross-referenced against Appendix A1.1's catch-name-to-group allocation table (PDF pp.37-41). This group is shared across the whole LME (Appendix A1.2: area proportion 1, 0.689 or 0.849 of the study area), so no region-choice ambiguity applies to it. | Guénette (2013), BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09; Table 16 (PDF p.24, group list, TL, habitat fraction, biomass, P/B, Q/B, EE) and Appendix A1 (PDF pp.37-45): A1.1 'Allocation of catch species to functional groups' (pp.37-41), A1.2 'Area considered for each functional group' (p.42), A1.3 'Functional groups composition' (pp.43-45). | None used beyond the focal paper and its own appendices; cross-checked against this project's own extracted model.json/REPORT.md (2026-09-04) for exact group names, order and trophic levels. | https://www.boblme.org/ (BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09; no DOI located for this report) | - | Species-composition evidence comes from the focal paper's own appendix (an in-document table, not an external supplement); no separate supplementary file exists for this report. Note the documented mass-balance defect on PDF p.27 (REPORT.md, 2026-09-04 extraction): the diet matrix's Detritus row is a verbatim duplicate of the Import row and prey rows 46-48 are missing entirely for consumers 30-41/46-48, leaving group 40 (3 Meiobenthos) with an empty diet column and groups 38/39/41 short of their detritus fraction; this affects diet composition, not the species-to-group membership used here. |

The Sea Around Us catch workbook mapped against this model carries a single LME-wide region_name/region_type with no country or sub-region breakdown per row, so every taxon assigned to a region-prefixed guild is pointed at its Region 2 instance specifically, chosen consistently over Region 1 and Region 3: Region 2 (Bangladesh, India, Sri Lanka) is the core Ganges-Brahmaputra-influenced Bay of Bengal shelf the LME is named for, hosts the two cross-regional flagship groups the paper singles out by name (2-3 Hilsa, 2-3 Indian mackerel), and is the coastal shelf best populated with demersal/coastal taxa -- unlike Region 1 (Maldives + high seas, only 0.005 of shelf area, almost entirely an oceanic large-pelagic fishery) or Region 3 (a more Southeast-Asian/Strait-of-Malacca-influenced shelf). This is a necessary modeling simplification forced by the workbook's lack of a region/country column, not a claim that the mapped catch actually originated in Region 2. | Guénette (2013), BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09; Table 16 (PDF p.24, group list, TL, habitat fraction, biomass, P/B, Q/B, EE) and Appendix A1 (PDF pp.37-45): A1.1 'Allocation of catch species to functional groups' (pp.37-41), A1.2 'Area considered for each functional group' (p.42), A1.3 'Functional groups composition' (pp.43-45). | None used beyond the focal paper and its own appendices; cross-checked against this project's own extracted model.json/REPORT.md (2026-09-04) for exact group names, order and trophic levels. | https://www.boblme.org/ (BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09; no DOI located for this report) | - | Species-composition evidence comes from the focal paper's own appendix (an in-document table, not an external supplement); no separate supplementary file exists for this report. Note the documented mass-balance defect on PDF p.27 (REPORT.md, 2026-09-04 extraction): the diet matrix's Detritus row is a verbatim duplicate of the Import row and prey rows 46-48 are missing entirely for consumers 30-41/46-48, leaving group 40 (3 Meiobenthos) with an empty diet column and groups 38/39/41 short of their detritus fraction; this affects diet composition, not the species-to-group membership used here. |

BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09, Bay of Bengal Large Marine Ecosystem Project, 62 pp. | Bay of Bengal (1978) | 3 S pelagics | 3.15 | Feeding guild/habitat (small coastal pelagic planktivores, excl. the two dedicated single-species groups) | Anodontostoma chacunda, Sardinella spp., Dussumieria elopsoides, Spratelloides spp., Amblygaster sirm, Escualosa thoracata, Bregmaceros mcclellandi, Coilia spp., Thryssa spp., Setipinna spp., Stolephorus spp., Encrasicholina spp., Lactarius lactarius, Pellona ditchela, Hemiramphus/exocoetid flyingfishes and halfbeaks (Appendix A1.3). | Explicit species/genus/family membership from Appendix A1.1 ('Allocation of catch species to functional groups', PDF pp.37-41) and Appendix A1.3 ('Functional groups composition', PDF pp.43-45), which give one composition list per functional-group name with no region qualifier (Appendix A1.2 confirms only the habitat-area fraction differs by region). BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09, Bay of Bengal Large Marine Ecosystem Project, 62 pp. | Bay of Bengal (1978) | 3 Zooplankton | 2 | Habitat/size (planktonic invertebrates) | copepods, fish and invertebrate eggs, euphausiids, chaetognaths, siphonophores (Appendix A1.3). | Explicit species/genus/family membership from Appendix A1.1 ('Allocation of catch species to functional groups', PDF pp.37-41) and Appendix A1.3 ('Functional groups composition', PDF pp.43-45), which give one composition list per functional-group name with no region qualifier (Appendix A1.2 confirms only the habitat-area fraction differs by region). Region-choice convention (applies to every region-prefixed group in this model): this is a single whole-LME Ecopath model in which most functional groups exist three times -- once per sub-region (1 = Maldives + open ocean, 2 = Bangladesh/India/Sri Lanka shelf, 3 = Myanmar/Thailand/Malaysia/Andaman & Nicobar/Sumatra shelf; p.7-9 of the source) -- rather than as separate models; each regional instance differs only in habitat-area fraction and biomass (Table 16, Appendix A1.2), not in species composition (Appendix A1.1/A1.3 give one composition list per functional-group name, with no region qualifier). The Sea Around Us catch workbook mapped against this model carries a single LME-wide region_name/region_type with no country or sub-region breakdown per row, so every taxon assigned to a region-prefixed guild is pointed at its Region 2 instance specifically, chosen consistently over Region 1 and Region 3: Region 2 (Bangladesh, India, Sri Lanka) is the core Ganges-Brahmaputra-influenced Bay of Bengal shelf the LME is named for, hosts the two cross-regional flagship groups the paper singles out by name (2-3 Hilsa, 2-3 Indian mackerel), and is the coastal shelf best populated with demersal/coastal taxa -- unlike Region 1 (Maldives + high seas, only 0.005 of shelf area, almost entirely an oceanic large-pelagic fishery) or Region 3 (a more Southeast-Asian/Strait-of-Malacca-influenced shelf). This is a necessary modeling simplification forced by the workbook's lack of a region/country column, not a claim that the mapped catch actually originated in Region 2. | Guénette (2013), BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09; Table 16 (PDF p.24, group list, TL, habitat fraction, biomass, P/B, Q/B, EE) and Appendix A1 (PDF pp.37-45): A1.1 'Allocation of catch species to functional groups' (pp.37-41), A1.2 'Area considered for each functional group' (p.42), A1.3 'Functional groups composition' (pp.43-45). | None used beyond the focal paper and its own appendices; cross-checked against this project's own extracted model.json/REPORT.md (2026-09-04) for exact group names, order and trophic levels. | https://www.boblme.org/ (BOBLME-2014-Ecology-09; no DOI located for this report) | - | Species-composition evidence comes from the focal paper's own appendix (an in-document table, not an external supplement); no separate supplementary file exists for this report.
